[Q1] He goes hard with one and all.
[EXAM: SSC Stenographer 10/12/2024 (Morning)]
[DIFFICULTY: Medium]
(a) To be busy over trifles
(b) To remain neutral
(c) To be difficult
(d) To be unreliable
[ANS] c
[SOL]
Goes hard - to be difficult.
💡 हिन्दी व्याख्या: इस मुहावरे का सही लाक्षणिक अर्थ (Figurative Meaning) 'To be difficult' है।

[Q4] Your guess is as good as mine
[EXAM: SSC Stenographer 10/12/2024 (Afternoon)]
[DIFFICULTY: Medium]
(a) To guess an incredible idea
(b) To have no idea of the answer
(c) To predict a disaster
(d) To be superstitious

[Q5] Loose cannon
[EXAM: SSC Stenographer 10/12/2024 (Afternoon)]
[DIFFICULTY: Medium]
(a) Someone who is quiet and reserved in social situations
(b) Someone who is unpredictable and liable to cause problems
(c) Someone who is enthusiastic and very eager to take responsibilities
(d) Someone who is well known and respected in society
[ANS] b
[SOL]
Loose canon - someone who is unpredictable and liable to cause problems. E.g.- The new manager is a loose cannon. He makes sudden decisions without consulting anyone
💡 हिन्दी व्याख्या: इस मुहावरे का सही लाक्षणिक अर्थ (Figurative Meaning) 'Someone who is unpredictable and liable to cause problems' है।

[Q47] Fair we at her friend

(a) high and mighty

Beside the mark - something Idioms is irrelevant or not to the point. (a) 
• Fish out of water - uncomfortable. (d) When life gives you lemons, make lemonade. - make the best out of difficult situations. (a) 
• Turned a deaf ear to - disregarded. E.g.- He turned a deaf ear to their complaints and continued with his plan. (d) 
• Straight shooter - a thoroughly upright straightforward person. (a) 
• Once in a blue moon - not very often. (c) Through thick and thin - under all conditions, no matter how challenging or difficult. (a) Keep the wolf from the door - avoid starvation. (b) Around the clock (idiom) - day and night. (c) 
• To bring to light - to disclose. E.g.- The investigation brought to light several previously unknown facts about the case. (c) 
• Pulled a long face - to look saddened. (c) 
• Easy does it - used to tell someone to do something slowly and carefully. E.g.- Easy does it with the boxes. Don't drop anything fragile. (c) fair-weather friend - 'Fair-weather friend' is an idiom which means 'a person who is dependable in good times but is not in times of trouble.' The given sentence states that I thought Sadhna would always stick by me, but when I got into trouble, she turned out to be a fair-weather friend. Hence, 'fair-weather friend' is the most appropriate answer. (d) Penelope's web - an endless task. (b) 
• On cloud nine - being very delighted. E.g.- She was on cloud nine after hearing about her promotion. (a) 
• A bitter pill - facing a distressing situation. E.g.- Accepting that I lost the competition was a bitter pill to swallow. (d) 
• Creature of habit - one who does same thing in the same way. E.g.- Tom is a creature of habit. He wakes up at the same time every day. (b) 
• Hit the roof - to become very angry. E.g.- When he saw the mess in the kitchen, he hit the roof. (c) 
• Henpecked husbands - oppressed husband. (a) 
• At a low ebb - in a depressed or weak state. E.g.- She felt at a low ebb after failing the exam. (a) 
• Off and on - now and then. E.g.- He visits his grandparents off and on throughout the year. (c) 
• Left out in the cold - to be ignored. E.g.- She felt left out in the cold when her friends didn't invite her to the party. (d) 
• Break the ice - to say or do something that makes people feel more relaxed, especially at the beginning of a meeting, party. E.g.- He told a joke to break the ice at the start of the meeting. (c) 
• Jaundiced eye - resentment. (a) 
• A rainy day - a time when money might unexpectedly be needed. E.g.- She saved some money for a rainy day, just in case of an emergency. (c) 
• Call a spade a spade - speak truthfully. (d) Pat myself on the back - to praise myself for doing something good. (c) 
• Maiden speech - first speech. (d) had no effect on 'Fell flat with' is an idiom which means to have no effect on the audience. The given passage states that the entire speech of the revivalist fell flat with the crowd that was present there. Hence, 'had no effect on' is the most appropriate answer. (d) the major portion 'The lion's share' is an idiom which means the major portion. Hence, option(d) is the most appropriate answer. (b) work harder than before 'Pull our socks up' is an idiom which means to work harder than before. Hence, option(b) is the most appropriate (b) Turned up their nose at her - treated her with contempt. (a) 
• Traitor - betrayer of the country. (d) 
• Let the dust settle - to wait for a situation to become clear or certain. E.g.- Let's wait a few days and let the dust settle before making any decisions. (c) Making a beeline (idiom) - going straight and quick. (d) The tip of the iceberg - a small, noticeable part of a problem, the total size of which is really much greater. (d) Kept her cards close to her chest - Kept her plans and thoughts hidden. (a) 
• High and dry - helpless. answer.
💡 हिन्दी व्याख्या: इस मुहावरे का सही लाक्षणिक अर्थ (Figurative Meaning) 'beside the mark' है।

(a) Mohit was so loyal and committed to his company that he rejected lucrative offers from different companies.

(c) To prove this is not a trap, I want to check the credibility of your plan.
(d) You are here just because you have recognised your hidden potential.
[ANS] b
[SOL]
Rachit had some important work and so he cancelled the show tickets for everyone. (b)
💡 हिन्दी व्याख्या: इस मुहावरे का सही लाक्षणिक अर्थ (Figurative Meaning) 'Rachit had some important work and so he cancelled the show tickets for everyone.' है।

(c) To predict the weather for tomorrow
(d) To schedule a meeting for tomorrow
[ANS] b
[SOL]
To call it a day means to stop working on a task or conclude the day's activities.
उदा. 'Call it a day' का अर्थ है काम बंद करना या आज के लिए कार्य समाप्त घोषित करना।

[EXAM: SSC CGL 2023 Tier-2]
[DIFFICULTY: Hard]
(a) Through physical growth and decay
(b) During good times and bad times alike
(c) By following strict rules and regulations
(d) Through dense forests and narrow paths
[ANS] b
[SOL]
Through thick and thin means under all circumstances, no matter how difficult; through good and bad times.
उदा. 'Through thick and thin' का अर्थ है हर परिस्थिति में, चाहे अच्छा समय हो या बुरा समय।

[Q756] break the ice
[EXAM: SSC CGL 2024 Tier-1]
[DIFFICULTY: Medium]

(b) To initiate interaction and ease tension
(c) To physically crack ice into pieces
(d) To end a conversation abruptly
[ANS] b
[SOL]
The idiom "break the ice" means to initiate conversation or activity that reduces tension. It is used when someone wants to make others feel comfortable.\nउदा. "He told a joke to break the ice at the meeting."
[RULE] Idioms / Figurative Language

[Q757] spill the beans
[EXAM: SSC CGL 2024 Tier-1]
[DIFFICULTY: Medium]
(a) To accidentally drop beans on the floor
(b) To reveal a secret or confidential information
(c) To eat too many beans
(d) To forget something important
[ANS] b
[SOL]
"Spill the beans" refers to unintentionally disclosing a secret or confidential information. It is commonly used when someone reveals something they were supposed to keep hidden.\nउदा. "He spilled the beans about the surprise party."
[RULE] Idioms / Figurative Language

---

[Q758] barking up the wrong tree
[EXAM: SSC CGL 2024 Tier-1]
[DIFFICULTY: Medium]
(a) To chase a dog that is not yours
(b) To pursue a mistaken or misguided course of action
(c) To bark loudly in a forest
(d) To search for something that is easily found
[ANS] b
[SOL]
"Barking up the wrong tree" indicates that someone is pursuing a mistaken or incorrect line of reasoning or action. It comes from hunting dogs mistakenly chasing a prey in the wrong tree.\nउदा. "She blamed me for the error, but I’m barking up the wrong tree."
[RULE] Idioms / Figurative Language

---

[Q759] the ball is in your court
[EXAM: SSC CGL 2024 Tier-1]
[DIFFICULTY: Medium]
(a) You have a basketball in your hand
(b) It is now your turn to take action
(c) You need to play a game of ball
(d) You are responsible for a court case
[ANS] b
[SOL]
"The ball is in your court" means the decision or responsibility now lies with you. It originates from tennis or basketball where the ball is in the opponent’s court.\nउदा. "I’ve done all I can; the ball is in your court."
[RULE] Idioms / Figurative Language

(d) To involve a long journey

"Cost an arm and a leg" means something is very expensive or costly in price. It does not refer to actual limbs.\nउदा. "The new smartphone costs an arm and a leg."

(c) To work on a construction site

[ANS] b

[Q762] under the weather

(d) To be influenced by global climate
[ANS] a

"Under the weather" means feeling sick or unwell. It is used to express poor health or a slight illness.\nउदा. "I’m feeling under the weather, so I’ll skip the meeting."
